Image name: m-_task2_gt1_PAIR3
Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: no_cot, Attempt: 5
--- Model Response ---
To solve this problem, we need to identify the minimal set of nodes that when removed, will disconnect the two black nodes (N and Q) from each other.

1. **Trace the Connections**:
   - Node N is connected to nodes S, E, F, T, and C.
   - Node Q is connected to nodes T, C, I, and J.

2. **Identify Overlapping Paths**:
   - Both N and Q are connected to nodes T and C.
   - Removing either T or C alone is not sufficient as there are multiple paths between N and Q.

3. **Find the Minimum Cut Set**:
   - To completely disconnect N and Q, we need to cut all paths connecting them.
   - Removing T and C will disconnect the paths through these nodes.
   - Additionally, removing either E or F will ensure that there is no alternative path left. Since removing either one separately may not suffice to disconnect all paths, let's remove both.

4. **Conclusion**:
   - The minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 3.
   - The cut set is [T, C, E].

Therefore, the answer is:

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [T, C, E]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 1, 'cut_set': [['H'], ['I']]}

     - Removing \( (I, H) \) disconnects \( I \) from the direct connection to \( H \) and subsequent paths.

   - \( (I, H) \) to eliminate \( I \)'s direct hub to other nodes reaching \( Q \).

Therefore:
**Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(I, H), (B, J)].**

   - Node G is connected to nodes T, C, I, H, and J.

   - Removing S, E, and F removes all direct and indirect connections between N and G including through intermediary nodes A, B, P, Q, T, C, I, and H.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to be removed is 3.

   - Path 5: R → F → B → N → H → Q → I
   - Path 6: R → F → B → N → T → C → O → G → H → I

### Finding the Minimum Cut Set:

We need to find the minimum number of edges to remove to disconnect all these paths.

- Removing the edge (F, B) will disconnect the paths that go through B.
- Removing the edge (H, I) will disconnect the paths that go through H.
- Removing the edge (N, H) will disconnect the paths that go through N.
- Removing the edge (N, T) will disconnect the paths that go through T.

By removing the edges (F, B) and (H, I), we can disconnect all paths between R and I.

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(F, B), (H, I)].
